{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"58479281-f0fc-437a-b3c2-51154bb8cfd1","agentId":"a95165ff517d07262","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"You are RED-PEN #2, an adversarial reviewer for the SIGIL spine at /home/kali/sigil (a git repo). Invariants brief: /tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/spine_design_brief.md.\n\nYOUR LENS — BEHAVIOR PARITY + CORRECTNESS + CONSUMER TRANSPARENCY. Attack that and the correctness of the new code:\n1. **Byte-identical parity.** In the legacy path (no manifest), is EVERY read/write path truly unchanged vs pre-Slice-0? Trace iter_records/get/tail/count/entries/verify/next_seq/_read_last_entry/_ensure_index/_scan_from now that they use self._active instead of self.path — with no manifest, self._active == self.path, so confirm they are identical. Does append behave identically (same record shape, same cert_digest, same fsync-before-ack)? Any NEW per-append cost or side effect (the manifest read in _refresh_active_under_lock, the _last reset) that changes observable behavior or ordering?\n2. **`_refresh_active_under_lock` in the common case.** It runs on EVERY append. When the active is unchanged, does it correctly do NOTHING beyond a manifest read (no spurious index invalidation, no _last reset that could drop a concurrently-updated tip)? Trace the `if new_active != self._active or (m is not None) != (self._manifest is not None)` condition for: legacy→legacy (m None), migrated→migrated (m same). Is there any case where it needlessly invalidates and re-reads the tip, and could that ever be WRONG (e.g. after another instance appended)?\n3. **The 34 consumers.** The design says exactly 3 consumers change in later slices and Slice 0 changes none. {"parentUuid":"1ed50c27-6944-4aed-a642-a06eeb01d1e2","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a95165ff517d07262","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- crucible: How to operate CRUCIBLE — the authorized, prove-don't-guess offensive-security engine in framework/v2. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- verify: Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.\n- code-review: Review the current diff for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud). Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. {"parentUuid":"ea41edce-9c37-4890-a9f7-f4194c2e2f84","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"58479281-f0fc-437a-b3c2-51154bb8cfd1","agentId":"a95165ff517d07262","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01G5KoXK35dmrvs9CRFtJXpZ","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# SIGIL spine bounded-growth — DESIGN BRIEF (distilled from the understand phase)\n2\t\n3\tThe SIGIL spine is an append-only, hash-chained, Ed25519-signed JSONL log at `~/.sigil/spine/spine.jsonl`\n4\t(93 MB / 43,412 records, growing). GOAL: bound its growth / per-file size / read latency **without\n5\tweakening tamper-evidence or the append-only guarantee**. 34 consumers across 464 call sites read it;\n6\t23 do FULL scans; 9 need old-seq resolution. Full raw analysis (invariants, per-consumer contract,\n7\ttamper-conflict, durability, critique) is in `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/tasks/w705miq65.output` — read it for detail. Code is at `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/spine/*` and `sigil/reuse/{chain,canonical,models}.py`.\n8\t\n9\t## The 19 invariants a design MUST preserve (condensed)\n10\t\n11\t1. **chain-anchored-from-genesis** — `verify_chain` seeds `prev=_GENESIS_PREV` and rejects entries[0] if\n12\t   its prev_hash != genesis. (It does NOT require entries[0].seq==0 — only prev==genesis + contiguity —\n13\t   so a rebased chain at seq K is valid IFF K's prev_hash is rewritten to a genesis-equivalent value.)\n14\t2. **strict-seq-contiguity** — seq[i]==seq[i-1]+1, no gaps/dupes. Prune may only remove a CONTIGUOUS\n15\t   PREFIX, never punch interior holes.\n16\t3. **seq-is-immutable-primary-key** — never renumber; entry_hash binds seq; parent_id/supersedes_id/\n17\t   cursors/graph anchors reference seq. Keep original seqs across any compaction.\n18\t4. **signed-head-entry_count-is-the-anti-truncation-gate** — `classify_head` flags `len(entries) <\n19\t   head.entry_count` as TAMPERING; entry_count is ABSOLUTE. THE central conflict: prune ⇒ shorter ⇒ reads\n20\t   as rollback. Any prune MUST re-sign a snapshot-aware head atomically.\n21\t5. **monotonic-last_seq anti-rollback** — tip seq never decreases (verify_head prev_highwater [dormant on\n22\t   checkpoint path]; SpineTailer._high_water in-memory). Front-prune keeps tip seq ⇒ safe; renumber ⇒\n23\t   false trip + real-truncation-indistinguishable.\n24\t6. **append-only / offsets-never-move / no-in-place-rewrite** — never rewrite in place; build-then-\n25\t   os.replace. In-place same-or-larger rewrite is UNDETECTED by the size-only index heuristic ⇒ silent\n26\t   wrong reads.\n27\t7. **torn-tail-truncate-before-append (BLOCK-1)** — must run on the ACTIVE append segment only; a sealed\n28\t   segment that ends torn will never be truncated by a future append ⇒ permanent false verify() gap\n29\t   (critique A2: rotation must run `_last_valid_boundary` truncate at SEAL time).\n30\t8. **fsync durability** — acked append fsync'd; head swapped via temp+fsync+os.replace+dir-fsync\n31\t   (`checkpoint._atomic_write_text`). Every rotation output must match this.\n32\t9. **index extend/rebuild correctness under external mutation** — detection is SIZE-ONLY today; must\n33\t   become IDENTITY/EPOCH-based (st_dev/st_ino + manifest generation), and segment-aware (key by\n34\t   (segment,offset)). Rotation must not read as an in-place shrink (which is a truncation attack).\n35\t10. **replay-stable content digest (ts excluded)** — any snapshot/manifest record must digest via the\n36\t    same `append()` content shape + canonical_json (ts excluded) or its binding check fails.\n37\t11. **two-layer unkeyed integrity vs the keyed signed head** — a compactor is a recompute-capable writer;\n38\t    verify() alone can't certify a compaction. The owner-signed head is the authority; compaction must be\n39\t    owner-key-gated.\n40\t12. **read-path presents ONE contiguous genesis chain** — entries()/iter_records(-1)/verify()/count()\n41\t    must transparently concatenate snapshot-front + sealed segments + active segment in seq order, no\n42\t    gap/dup at a seam, so all 34 consumers keep working unchanged.\n43\t13. **durable tip determines next_seq (no seq reuse)** — `_read_last_entry`/next_seq must resolve the tip\n44\t    from the ACTIVE segment (true global max seq).\n45\t14. **cross-writer serialization must cover the compactor** — spine_lock (RLock) + flock. CRITICAL: flock\n46\t    binds to the INODE, not the path; os.replace swaps the inode, so flock-on-target CANNOT exclude a\n47\t    cross-process appender. Need a PATH-STABLE lockfile (`spine.lock`) both append & compaction flock\n48\t    before resolving the active segment.\n49\t15. **monotonic-state-by-full-scan must survive** — per-device nonce high-water (envelope.py), newest\n50\t    warden_checkpoint per pubkey (cli.py), account.create cap count (actor_scope/actor), device\n51\t    authorize/revoke ledger (mesh/registry), kill-switch latch (killswitch), promotion grants\n52\t    (promotion), unresolved queued approvals (approvals), archivist supersession + promotion-key ledger\n53\t    (revise), open threads/commitments/contradictions + citations (queries.py). Rotation (retain-all)\n54\t    keeps these IF scans span segments; PRUNE would silently corrupt them unless each is folded into the\n55\t    signed snapshot.\n56\t16. **referential integrity of parent_id/supersedes_id across a prune** — never dangle; never mutate a\n57\t    retained record's parent/supersedes (breaks its binding).\n58\t17. **canonical/domain primitives frozen** — snapshot/head schema changes need a schema_version bump +\n59\t    migration, computed by the same reuse functions.\n60\t18. **single signed head is the commit point** — extend to carry a snapshot base coherently; keep ONE\n61\t    atomically-published head.\n62\t19. **genesis-vs-empty count semantics** — a compacted store is never \"empty\"; next_seq/last_seq must\n63\t    derive from the snapshot base, not physical len==0.\n64\t\n65\t## Critique additions (must be in any design)\n66\t- **A1 closed head schema** — `SignedChainHead`/`ChainEntry` are `extra=\"forbid\"`. A v2 head with snapshot\n67\t  fields is a BREAKING wire migration; today the two head-consumers fail DIFFERENTLY (SpineTailer swallows\n68\t  ⇒ silent un-anchored; verify_checkpoint has no try/except ⇒ crashes `sigil verify` + mcp ingest_status).\n69\t- **A3 next_seq is cached** — a long-lived store instance compacted by another process keeps stale\n70\t  next_seq; must invalidate on epoch change.\n71\t- **A4 size-as-change-token in THREE places** — store._ensure_index, killswitch.py:57, gesture/session.py:121.\n72\t  A same-size rotation serves a STALE kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction. Fix must be UNIFIED\n73\t  (shared epoch token).\n74\t- **Missing consumers** — consolidate/queries.py (full-scan, citation-heavy MCP surface — first-class),\n75\t  consolidate/gate.py (get(seq) grounding), ingest/transcript.py (sets parent_id ⇒ referential integrity).\n76\t- **C1 (decisive)** — NO durable anti-rollback floor lives OUTSIDE the compactable data. Hard-prune turns\n77\t  the `n<signed_n` truncation alarm into a no-op for the pruned range, so a cold/fresh verifier can't\n78\t  distinguish a legit compaction-baseline from a replayed old (short-spine, low-count-head) pair. Closing\n79\t  this needs a durable external floor (per-verifier local last-accepted last_seq, and/or authenticated\n80\t  head delivery a mirror can't roll back). WARM same-host verifiers are fine; COLD/fresh are not.\n81\t- **C2** — once classify_head slices only the LIVE window, the pruned prefix is NO LONGER re-hashed, so the\n82\t  snapshot's committed digest MUST be a Merkle/hash commitment binding EVERY pruned record (else a pre-\n83\t  archival edit is unprovable). This is an invariant, not a nice-to-have.\n84\t- **D1** — must NOT hold the append lock across the O(file) heavy copy (would block a kill-switch PANIC\n85\t  append). Heavy copy of immutable records is lock-free; only a bounded seal→publish critical section\n86\t  takes the lock.\n87\t- **D2** — live-reader vs unlink race across the manifest indirection: readers must snapshot ALL segment\n88\t  fds right after reading the manifest; unlink deferred behind a reader grace/refcount.\n89\t\n90\t## Cutover protocol (durability analysis — the required shape)\n91\tONE authoritative **manifest** naming the active segment set = the sole linearization point, swapped via\n92\t`_atomic_write_text` (temp→fsync→os.replace→dir-fsync). Ordering: write+fsync new segment(s) → write+fsync\n93\tnew manifest → os.replace manifest (the atomic instant) → dir-fsync → ONLY THEN unlink superseded segments.\n94\tentries()/count() derive the set from the MANIFEST, never a directory glob (glob double-counts during the\n95\tunlink window). Manifest swap + head swap must be crash-consistent together (a crash between them yields a\n96\tfalse TAMPERING window). Seal-time torn-tail truncate on the sealed segment. Carry seq/entry_hash across\n97\tthe seam so verify_chain's contiguity+linkage never break.\n98\t\n99\t## The 5 questions the DESIGN must answer\n100\t1. Where does the durable, non-replayable anti-rollback floor live? (C1) — only relevant if PRUNING.\n101\t2. Exact SignedChainHead schema migration + version-negotiation (old mobile clients / sigil verify vs a v2 head)?\n102\t3. The atomic multi-file commit protocol + which bounded critical section holds the lock (D1/D2/D3)?\n103\t4. Which record classes are un-prunable state-bearers and how is each folded into the signed snapshot? (only if pruning)\n104\t5. Honest contract for resolving a pruned seq (positive \"compacted below N, see snapshot X\" vs a bare 404)?\n105\t\n106\t## Key strategic observation (for the panel to validate or challenge)\n107\tRetain-all SEGMENT ROTATION needs NONE of {C1 durable floor, snapshot state-folding, head-schema prune\n108\tsemantics} — it keeps entry_count absolute, the chain genesis-rooted, all 34 consumers transparent (via a\n109\tsegment-spanning read API), and reclaims practical disk via gzip-on-seal (JSONL ~5-10x). It touches only\n110\tstorage mechanics (manifest, lockfile, epoch-index, cutover) — the HARD-but-BOUNDED engineering. HARD-PRUNE\n111\t(space reclaim by deleting records) is where C1 + state-folding + a breaking head migration live — a\n112\ttamper-evidence tradeoff that is an OPERATOR decision, not an autonomous one.\n113\t"}]},"uuid":"6a125db7-a3cd-4d12-aa00-239d27a07fa3","timestamp":"2026-07-19T15:53:25.379Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ea41edce-9c37-4890-a9f7-f4194c2e2f84","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Slugified cwd = dir name under ~/.claude/projects.\n59\tdiff --git a/sigil/spine/atomicio.py b/sigil/spine/atomicio.py\n60\tnew file mode 100644\n61\tindex 0000000..8891cfb\n62\t--- /dev/null\n63\t+++ b/sigil/spine/atomicio.py\n64\t@@ -0,0 +1,48 @@\n65\t+\"\"\"Durable, atomic file replacement — the one implementation the spine's crash-safe swaps share.\n66\t+\n67\t+Extracted verbatim from `checkpoint._atomic_write_text` (FIX 3) so the signed head, the manifest, and\n68\t+any future single-file cutover all use ONE audited routine rather than drifting copies. The contract:\n69\t+a reader observes either the whole old file or the whole new file (never a torn one), and the new\n70\t+content survives a crash at any point — a crash mid-write leaves the PREVIOUS valid file intact.\n71\t+\"\"\"\n72\t+from __future__ import annotations\n73\t+\n74\t+import os\n75\t+import tempfile\n76\t+from pathlib import Path\n77\t+\n78\t+\n79\t+def fsync_dir(directory: Path | str) -> None:\n80\t+    \"\"\"fsync a directory so a rename/create inside it is itself durable across a crash. A no-op-on-error\n81\t+    on filesystems that don't support directory fsync (the rename still lands; only its durability window\n82\t+    widens).\"\"\"\n83\t+    try:\n84\t+        dfd = os.open(str(directory), os.O_DIRECTORY)\n85\t+        try:\n86\t+            os.fsync(dfd)\n87\t+        finally:\n88\t+            os.close(dfd)\n89\t+    except OSError:  # pragma: no cover — dir fsync unsupported on some filesystems\n90\t+        pass\n91\t+\n92\t+\n93\t+def atomic_write_text(path: Path | str, data: str, *, prefix: str = \".tmp-\") -> None:\n94\t+    \"\"\"Durably + atomically replace `path` with `data`: write a temp file in the SAME dir, fsync it,\n95\t+    `os.replace()` over the target (atomic on POSIX), then fsync the directory so the rename survives a\n96\t+    crash. A crash at any point leaves the previous valid file intact, never a partially-written one.\"\"\"\n97\t+    path = Path(path)\n98\t+    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n99\t+    fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=str(path.parent), prefix=prefix, suffix=\".tmp\")\n100\t+    try:\n101\t+        with os.fdopen(fd, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n102\t+            f.write(data)\n103\t+            f.flush()\n104\t+            os.fsync(f.fileno())\n105\t+        os.replace(tmp, path)\n106\t+    except BaseException:\n107\t+        try:\n108\t+            os.unlink(tmp)\n109\t+        except OSError:\n110\t+            pass\n111\t+        raise\n112\t+    fsync_dir(path.parent)\n113\tdiff --git a/sigil/spine/checkpoint.py b/sigil/spine/checkpoint.py\n114\tindex b2dfa98..dc26460 100644\n115\t--- a/sigil/spine/checkpoint.py\n116\t+++ b/sigil/spine/checkpoint.py\n117\t@@ -12,7 +12,6 @@ added later by raising the trust-root threshold.\n118\t from __future__ import annotations\n119\t \n120\t import os\n121\t-import tempfile\n122\t from pathlib import Path\n123\t \n124\t from ..config import HEAD_PATH, KEYS_DIR, OWNER_KEY_ID, SCOPE\n125\t@@ -24,6 +23,7 @@ from ..reuse import (\n126\t     sign_head,\n127\t     verify_head,\n128\t )\n129\t+from .atomicio import atomic_write_text\n130\t from .store import SpineStore\n131\t \n132\t _PRIV = KEYS_DIR / \"owner.priv\"\n133\t@@ -31,33 +31,11 @@ _PUB = KEYS_DIR / \"owner.pub\"\n134\t \n135\t \n136\t def _atomic_write_text(path: Path, data: str) -> None:\n137\t-    \"\"\"Durably + atomically replace `path` with `data` (FIX 3): write a temp file in the SAME dir,\n138\t-    fsync it, `os.replace()` over the target (atomic on POSIX — a reader sees either the old or the\n139\t-    new head, never a torn one), then fsync the directory so the rename itself survives a crash. Delegates to\n166\t+    the one shared `spine.atomicio.atomic_write_text` so the head, the manifest, and every cutover use the\n167\t+    same audited routine (no drift).\"\"\"\n168\t+    atomic_write_text(path, data, prefix=\".head-\")\n169\t \n170\t \n171\t def _owner_keys() -> tuple[str, str]:\n172\tdiff --git a/sigil/spine/manifest.py b/sigil/spine/manifest.py\n173\tnew file mode 100644\n174\tindex 0000000..ce88a68\n175\t--- /dev/null\n176\t+++ b/sigil/spine/manifest.py\n177\t@@ -0,0 +1,127 @@\n178\t+\"\"\"The spine's segment MANIFEST — the sole linearization point for the retain-all segment set.\n179\t+\n180\t+The spine is stored as a sequence of size-bounded, immutable **sealed** segments plus exactly one\n181\t+**active** (append) segment; the manifest names them in seq order. It is deliberately UNSIGNED and\n182\t+NON-LOAD-BEARING: record order, count, and every tamper decision are re-derived from the segment BYTES\n183\t+(via `verify_chain`/`classify_head`), never from the manifest's convenience fields. A doctored manifest\n184\t+therefore fails CLOSED — dropping/reordering an interior segment breaks `prev_hash`/seq contiguity, a\n185\t+dropped tail shortens `entries()` (the signed head's `n < entry_count` gate fires), a dropped front fails\n186\t+the genesis check. (A future signed-manifest tier would set `manifest_sig` — reserved `null` — because it\n187\t+would then attest pruned ranges; retain-all needs no such trust.)\n188\t+\n189\t+`SpineLayout` derives the whole on-disk layout from the spine data-file path a `SpineStore` is constructed\n190\t+with, so a store on a temp path is fully isolated. This module does no locking — the caller (`SpineStore`)\n191\t+holds the cross-process flock while reading/writing/migrating.\n192\t+\"\"\"\n193\t+from __future__ import annotations\n194\t+\n195\t+from dataclasses import dataclass\n196\t+from pathlib import Path\n197\t+\n198\t+from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field\n199\t+\n200\t+from ..reuse.chain import _GENESIS_PREV\n201\t+from .atomicio import atomic_write_text\n202\t+\n203\t+SEGMENT_STEM = \"seg-\"\n204\t+_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1\n205\t+\n206\t+\n207\t+def segment_filename(seg_id: int, codec: str = \"none\") -> str:\n208\t+    \"\"\"Zero-padded segment file name; `.gz` suffix when gzip-sealed (Slice 4).\"\"\"\n209\t+    base = f\"{SEGMENT_STEM}{seg_id:08d}.jsonl\"\n210\t+    return base + \".gz\" if codec == \"gzip\" else base\n211\t+\n212\t+\n213\t+class Segment(BaseModel):\n214\t+    \"\"\"One segment in the manifest. `first_prev_hash`/`boundary_hash` are the chain-linkage the reader\n215\t+    CROSS-CHECKS against the segment's real first/last records — they are convenience, not authority.\"\"\"\n216\t+    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"ignore\")   # forward-compatible: tolerate fields a newer writer added\n217\t+    id: int = Field(ge=0)\n218\t+    file: str                                    # path RELATIVE to the spine dir, e.g. \"segments/seg-00000000.jsonl\"\n219\t+    codec: str = \"none\"                          # \"none\" | \"gzip\"\n220\t+    sealed: bool = False\n221\t+    first_seq: int = Field(ge=0)\n222\t+    last_seq: int | None = None                  # null while active\n223\t+    count: int | None = None                     # null while active; CONVENIENCE ONLY — never a tamper input\n224\t+    first_prev_hash: str = _GENESIS_PREV         # == the prior segment's boundary_hash (seg 0 == genesis)\n225\t+    boundary_hash: str | None = None             # == entry_hash(last_seq); null while active\n226\t+    bytes: int = 0\n227\t+    sha256: str | None = None                    # at-rest file integrity; NOT a chain digest\n228\t+\n229\t+\n230\t+class Manifest(BaseModel):\n231\t+    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"ignore\")\n232\t+    schema_version: int = _SCHEMA_VERSION\n233\t+    generation: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)     # monotonic epoch; ++ on EVERY swap — the unified change token\n234\t+    scope: str = \"\"\n235\t+    segments: list[Segment] = Field(default_factory=list)\n236\t+    manifest_sig: str | None = None              # reserved for the deferred signed-manifest tier\n237\t+\n238\t+    def active(self) -> Segment | None:\n239\t+        \"\"\"The single unsealed (append) segment, if any. There is at most one.\"\"\"\n240\t+        act = [s for s in self.segments if not s.sealed]\n241\t+        return act[-1] if act else None\n242\t+\n243\t+    def sealed_in_order(self) -> list[Segment]:\n244\t+        return sorted((s for s in self.segments if s.sealed), key=lambda s: s.first_seq)\n245\t+\n246\t+    def ordered(self) -> list[Segment]:\n247\t+        \"\"\"All segments in seq order (sealed by first_seq, then the active tail).\"\"\"\n248\t+        out = self.sealed_in_order()\n249\t+        act = self.active()\n250\t+        if act is not None:\n251\t+            out.append(act)\n252\t+        return out\n253\t+\n254\t+\n255\t+@dataclass(frozen=True)\n256\t+class SpineLayout:\n257\t+    \"\"\"The on-disk layout derived from a spine data-file path (…/spine.jsonl). Every path is relative to\n258\t+    that file's directory so a store on a temp path is isolated from the real ~/.sigil deployment.\"\"\"\n259\t+    data_path: Path            # the legacy/identity path (…/spine.jsonl) — the stable lock/identity token\n260\t+    spine_dir: Path\n261\t+    segments_dir: Path\n262\t+    manifest_path: Path\n263\t+    lockfile_path: Path\n264\t+    trash_dir: Path\n265\t+\n266\t+    @classmethod\n267\t+    def for_path(cls, data_path: Path | str) -> \"SpineLayout\":\n268\t+        p = Path(data_path)\n269\t+        d = p.parent\n270\t+        return cls(\n271\t+            data_path=p, spine_dir=d, segments_dir=d / \"segments\",\n272\t+            manifest_path=d / \"manifest.json\", lockfile_path=d / \"spine.lock\", trash_dir=d / \"trash\",\n273\t+        )\n274\t+\n275\t+    def seg_path(self, seg: Segment) -> Path:\n276\t+        \"\"\"Absolute path of a segment's file (its `file` is stored relative to the spine dir).\"\"\"\n277\t+        return self.spine_dir / seg.file\n278\t+\n279\t+\n280\t+def read_manifest(layout: SpineLayout) -> Manifest | None:\n281\t+    \"\"\"Load the manifest, or None if absent. A present-but-unparseable manifest RAISES (fail closed) —\n282\t+    the atomic write path guarantees it is never torn, so a parse failure means genuine corruption, not a\n283\t+    partial write, and must never be silently treated as 'no manifest' (which would strand the segments).\"\"\"\n284\t+    mp = layout.manifest_path\n285\t+    if not mp.exists():\n286\t+        return None\n287\t+    return Manifest.model_validate_json(mp.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n288\t+\n289\t+\n290\t+def write_manifest(layout: SpineLayout, manifest: Manifest) -> None:\n291\t+    \"\"\"Atomically publish the manifest (temp→fsync→os.replace→dir-fsync). THE cutover commit instant.\"\"\"\n292\t+    layout.manifest_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n293\t+    atomic_write_text(layout.manifest_path, manifest.model_dump_json(), prefix=\".manifest-\")\n294\t+\n295\t+\n296\t+def initial_manifest(scope: str, *, active_file: str, first_seq: int = 0,\n297\t+                     first_prev_hash: str = _GENESIS_PREV) -> Manifest:\n298\t+    \"\"\"A single-active-segment manifest (generation 0) — the shape produced by a fresh store or a\n299\t+    migration of an existing single-file spine.\"\"\"\n300\t+    return Manifest(\n301\t+        generation=0, scope=scope,\n302\t+        segments=[Segment(id=0, file=active_file, codec=\"none\", sealed=False,\n303\t+                          first_seq=first_seq, first_prev_hash=first_prev_hash)],\n304\t+    )\n305\tdiff --git a/sigil/spine/store.py b/sigil/spine/store.py\n306\tindex 0ce9a79..1092567 100644\n307\t--- a/sigil/spine/store.py\n308\t+++ b/sigil/spine/store.py\n309\t@@ -12,11 +12,21 @@ import json\n310\t import logging\n311\t import os\n312\t import threading\n313\t+from contextlib import contextmanager\n314\t from pathlib import Path\n315\t from typing import Any, Iterator\n316\t \n317\t from ..config import SCOPE, SPINE_PATH\n318\t from ..reuse import ChainEntry, append_entry, build_chain, digest_payload, verify_chain\n319\t+from .atomicio import fsync_dir\n320\t+from .manifest import (\n321\t+    Manifest,\n322\t+    SpineLayout,\n323\t+    initial_manifest,\n324\t+    read_manifest,\n325\t+    segment_filename,\n326\t+    write_manifest,\n327\t+)\n328\t from .models import SpineRecord, now_iso\n329\t \n330\t try:\n331\t@@ -123,13 +133,27 @@ def _last_valid_boundary(path: Path) -> \"tuple[int, ChainEntry | None]\":\n332\t \n333\t class SpineStore:\n334\t     def __init__(self, path: Path | str = SPINE_PATH) -> None:\n335\t+        # `self.path` is the STABLE identity + lock token (…/spine.jsonl). After a migration it is renamed\n336\t+        # away and no longer a data file, but it remains the key for the in-process RLock and the anchor\n337\t+        # the whole layout derives from — so the RLock that `envelope.consume` shares with `append` stays\n338\t+        # the same object across a rotation (invariant 14). All DATA I/O targets `self._active` (the\n339\t+        # manifest's active segment, or — with no manifest yet — the legacy single file in place, which is\n340\t+        # byte-identical to the pre-rotation store).\n341\t         self.path = Path(path)\n342\t         self.path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n343\t+        self._layout = SpineLayout.for_path(self.path)\n344\t+        # NB: `segments/` is created only when actually migrating/rotating (not on every construction), so a\n345\t+        # legacy/fresh store leaves the spine dir byte-identical to today (no stray empty dirs).\n346\t+        self._manifest: Manifest | None = read_manifest(self._layout)\n347\t+        self._reconcile_orphan_migration()          # complete a migrate() interrupted before the manifest write\n348\t+        self._active: Path = self._resolve_active_path()\n349\t         # seq -> byte-offset index (FIX 1). Full scans (verify/count/entries/iter_records(-1)) never touch it, staying a\n353\t         # single byte-identical pass. `_index_lock` guards the dict against concurrent read+append.\n354\t+        # Slice 0/1: the index is single-segment (over `self._active`); it is (segment, offset)-aware in\n355\t+        # a later slice. It is invalidated whenever the active segment changes under us.\n356\t         self._index_lock = threading.Lock()\n357\t         self._index_built = False\n358\t         self._offsets: dict[int, int] = {}\n359\t@@ -137,6 +161,113 @@ class SpineStore:\n360\t         self._scan_pos = 0                          # byte offset just past the last COMPLETE line indexed\n361\t         self._last: ChainEntry | None = self._read_last_entry()\n362\t \n363\t+    # --- segment layout / manifest ------------------------------------------------\n364\t+    def _resolve_active_path(self) -> Path:\n365\t+        \"\"\"The current append/read target. With a manifest, the active segment's absolute path; without\n366\t+        one (a fresh or not-yet-migrated legacy spine), the legacy single file in place — byte-identical\n367\t+        to the pre-rotation store, so nothing destructive happens until an explicit `migrate()`.\"\"\"\n368\t+        if self._manifest is not None:\n369\t+            act = self._manifest.active()\n370\t+            if act is not None:\n371\t+                return self._layout.seg_path(act)\n372\t+        return self.path\n373\t+\n374\t+    @contextmanager\n375\t+    def _crossproc_lock(self) -> \"Iterator[None]\":\n376\t+        \"\"\"Cross-process exclusion on the inode-stable lockfile (invariant 14 / D3). flock binds to the\n377\t+        open file DESCRIPTION, not the path, so an os.replace of the manifest/segments cannot move the\n378\t+        lock out from under a concurrent writer — which the prior flock-on-the-data-fd could not survive.\n379\t+        Best-effort exactly as before (fcntl absent on non-POSIX; a flock failure is swallowed).\"\"\"\n380\t+        if fcntl is None:  # pragma: no cover — non-POSIX\n381\t+            yield\n382\t+            return\n383\t+        self._layout.lockfile_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n384\t+        fd = os.open(str(self._layout.lockfile_path), os.O_RDWR | os.O_CREAT, 0o600)\n385\t+        try:\n386\t+            try:\n387\t+                fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX)\n388\t+            except OSError:  # pragma: no cover\n389\t+                pass\n390\t+            yield\n391\t+        finally:\n392\t+            os.close(fd)\n393\t+\n394\t+    def _refresh_active_under_lock(self) -> None:\n395\t+        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`. Drops the single-segment\n413\t+        index so the next read rebuilds it against the (possibly changed) active segment.\"\"\"\n414\t+        with self._index_lock:\n415\t+            self._index_built = False\n416\t+            self._offsets = {}\n417\t+            self._max_seq = -1\n418\t+            self._scan_pos = 0\n419\t+\n420\t+    def _reconcile_orphan_migration(self) -> None:\n421\t+        \"\"\"Close the migrate() crash window. migrate() does an ATOMIC `os.replace(spine.jsonl →\n422\t+        segments/seg-00000000.jsonl)` and THEN publishes the manifest; a crash BETWEEN them leaves seg-0\n423\t+        present, `spine.jsonl` gone, and no manifest — at which point the legacy fallback (active =\n424\t+        spine.jsonl) would read the spine as EMPTY and silently hide all history. The rename is atomic and\n425\t+        already durable, so if we observe exactly that state we simply finish the interrupted migration by\n426\t+        publishing the manifest (idempotent, under the cross-process lock). (os.replace is atomic, so\n427\t+        spine.jsonl and seg-0 are never both present or both absent from a torn rename — this one state is\n428\t+        the whole window.)\"\"\"\n429\t+        if self._manifest is not None:\n430\t+            return\n431\t+        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n432\t+        if not (seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists()):\n433\t+            return\n434\t+        with self._crossproc_lock():\n435\t+            if read_manifest(self._layout) is None and seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists():\n436\t+                write_manifest(self._layout,\n437\t+                               initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=f\"segments/{segment_filename(0)}\"))\n438\t+                _log.warning(\"spine: completed an interrupted migration — published the manifest for %s\", seg0)\n439\t+            self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n440\t+\n441\t+    def migrate(self) -> bool:\n442\t+        \"\"\"Move a legacy single-file spine into the segment layout: rename `spine.jsonl` →\n443\t+        `segments/seg-00000000.jsonl` (atomic, same filesystem — O(1), no byte copy) and publish a\n444\t+        single-active-segment manifest. Appends\n7\tare O(1): read the last line's entry, `append_entry`, write.\n8\t\"\"\"\n9\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n10\t\n11\timport json\n12\timport logging\n13\timport os\n14\timport threading\n15\tfrom contextlib import contextmanager\n16\tfrom pathlib import Path\n17\tfrom typing import Any, Iterator\n18\t\n19\tfrom ..config import SCOPE, SPINE_PATH\n20\tfrom ..reuse import ChainEntry, append_entry, build_chain, digest_payload, verify_chain\n21\tfrom .atomicio import fsync_dir\n22\tfrom .manifest import (\n23\t    Manifest,\n24\t    SpineLayout,\n25\t    initial_manifest,\n26\t    read_manifest,\n27\t    segment_filename,\n28\t    write_manifest,\n29\t)\n30\tfrom .models import SpineRecord, now_iso\n31\t\n32\ttry:\n33\t    import fcntl  # POSIX advisory file lock — cross-PROCESS append serialization\n34\texcept ImportError:  # pragma: no cover — non-POSIX\n35\t    fcntl = None  # type: ignore[assignment]\n36\t\n37\t_log = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n38\t\n39\t# The record fields SpineRecord.from_dict needs; a line missing any of these is corrupt (skipped by\n40\t# reads, so a mid-file gap still surfaces via verify()).\n41\t_REQUIRED_KEYS = (\"seq\", \"scope\", \"kind\", \"source\", \"actor\", \"cert_digest\", \"prev_hash\", \"entry_hash\")\n42\t\n43\t_LOCKS_GUARD = threading.Lock()\n44\t_LOCKS: dict[str, \"threading.RLock\"] = {}\n45\t\n46\t\n47\tdef spine_lock(path: Path | str) -> \"threading.RLock\":\n48\t    \"\"\"A process-wide RE-ENTRANT lock per resolved spine path. Serializes `append` (read-tip → write)\n49\t    across threads so concurrent writers can't fork the hash chain, and — being re-entrant — lets a\n50\t    caller make a check-then-append atomic (e.g. the nonce replay gate) while its inner `append` still\n51\t    acquires the same lock. Cross-PROCESS serialization is added by an flock inside `append`.\"\"\"\n52\t    key = str(Path(path).resolve())\n53\t    with _LOCKS_GUARD:\n54\t        lk = _LOCKS.get(key)\n55\t        if lk is None:\n56\t            lk = _LOCKS[key] = threading.RLock()\n57\t        return lk\n58\t\n59\t\n60\tdef _last_nonempty_line(path: Path) -> str | None:\n61\t    \"\"\"Read the last VALID record line without loading the file (seek-from-end). A torn/garbage tail\n62\t    line — a partial write from a crash — is SKIPPED so a read/restart never blows up on it: we return\n63\t    the last line that JSON-parses and carries the chain fields (FIX 2). None if no valid line exists.\n64\t    For a clean file this returns exactly the last non-empty line, byte-for-byte as before.\"\"\"\n65\t    try:\n66\t        size = path.stat().st_size\n67\t    except OSError:\n68\t        return None\n69\t    if size == 0:\n70\t        return None\n71\t    window = 8192\n72\t    while True:\n73\t        start = max(0, size - window)\n74\t        with path.open(\"rb\") as f:\n75\t            f.seek(start)\n76\t            buf = f.read(size - start)\n77\t        lines = [ln for ln in buf.split(b\"\\n\") if ln.strip()]\n78\t        if start > 0 and lines:\n79\t            lines = lines[1:]                       # the first line may be partial unless we are at BOF\n80\t        for raw in reversed(lines):\n81\t            try:\n82\t                d = json.loads(raw)\n83\t            except ValueError:\n84\t                d = None\n85\t            if isinstance(d, dict) and all(k in d for k in _REQUIRED_KEYS):\n86\t                return raw.decode(\"utf-8\")\n87\t            _log.warning(\"spine: skipping malformed tail line while seeking the tip (%s)\", path)\n88\t        if start == 0:\n89\t            return None                             # scanned the whole file, no valid line\n90\t        window *= 4                                 # a torn tail bigger than the window (rare) — widen\n91\t\n92\t\n93\tdef _last_valid_boundary(path: Path) -> \"tuple[int, ChainEntry | None]\":\n94\t    \"\"\"Return (byte offset just past the last VALID record line, its ChainEntry) — bytes BEYOND that\n95\t    offset are a torn tail from an interrupted write, which `append` truncates before writing so the new\n96\t    record cannot merge with them and be silently lost (BLOCK-1). (0, None) for an empty / all-torn file.\n97\t    For a clean file the offset equals the file size (nothing to truncate).\"\"\"\n98\t    try:\n99\t        size = path.stat().st_size\n100\t    except OSError:\n101\t        return 0, None\n102\t    if size == 0:\n103\t        return 0, None\n104\t    window = 8192\n105\t    while True:\n106\t        start = max(0, size - window)\n107\t        with path.open(\"rb\") as f:\n108\t            f.seek(start)\n109\t            buf = f.read(size - start)\n110\t        segs: list[tuple[int, bytes]] = []          # (byte offset just past this line's \\n, line bytes)\n111\t        i = 0\n112\t        while True:\n113\t            nl = buf.find(b\"\\n\", i)\n114\t            if nl == -1:\n115\t                break\n116\t            segs.append((start + nl + 1, buf[i:nl]))\n117\t            i = nl + 1\n118\t        for end, raw in reversed(segs):             # near-EOF first; front partials (start>0) never win\n119\t            if not raw.strip():\n120\t                continue\n121\t            try:\n122\t                d = json.loads(raw)\n123\t            except ValueError:\n124\t                d = None\n125\t            if isinstance(d, dict) and all(k in d for k in _REQUIRED_KEYS):\n126\t                return end, ChainEntry(seq=d[\"seq\"], prev_hash=d[\"prev_hash\"],\n127\t                                       cert_digest=d[\"cert_digest\"], entry_hash=d[\"entry_hash\"])\n128\t            _log.warning(\"spine: skipping malformed tail segment while seeking the tip (%s)\", path)\n129\t        if start == 0:\n130\t            return 0, None\n131\t        window *= 4\n132\t\n133\t\n134\tclass SpineStore:\n135\t    def __init__(self, path: Path | str = SPINE_PATH) -> None:\n136\t        # `self.path` is the STABLE identity + lock token (…/spine.jsonl). After a migration it is renamed\n137\t        # away and no longer a data file, but it remains the key for the in-process RLock and the anchor\n138\t        # the whole layout derives from — so the RLock that `envelope.consume` shares with `append` stays\n139\t        # the same object across a rotation (invariant 14). All DATA I/O targets `self._active` (the\n140\t        # manifest's active segment, or — with no manifest yet — the legacy single file in place, which is\n141\t        # byte-identical to the pre-rotation store).\n142\t        self.path = Path(path)\n143\t        self.path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n144\t        self._layout = SpineLayout.for_path(self.path)\n145\t        # NB: `segments/` is created only when actually migrating/rotating (not on every construction), so a\n146\t        # legacy/fresh store leaves the spine dir byte-identical to today (no stray empty dirs).\n147\t        self._manifest: Manifest | None = read_manifest(self._layout)\n148\t        self._reconcile_orphan_migration()          # complete a migrate() interrupted before the manifest write\n149\t        self._active: Path = self._resolve_active_path()\n150\t        # seq -> byte-offset index (FIX 1). Built lazily on the first TARGETED read (get / iter_records\n151\t        # with since_seq >= 0) and then kept current: maintained O(1) on our own appends, and extended\n152\t        # (never rewritten — an append-only file's offsets never move) when a stat shows another PROCESS\n153\t        # grew the file. Full scans (verify/count/entries/iter_records(-1)) never touch it, staying a\n154\t        # single byte-identical pass. `_index_lock` guards the dict against concurrent read+append.\n155\t        # Slice 0/1: the index is single-segment (over `self._active`); it is (segment, offset)-aware in\n156\t        # a later slice. It is invalidated whenever the active segment changes under us.\n157\t        self._index_lock = threading.Lock()\n158\t        self._index_built = False\n159\t        self._offsets: dict[int, int] = {}\n160\t        self._max_seq = -1\n161\t        self._scan_pos = 0                          # byte offset just past the last COMPLETE line indexed\n162\t        self._last: ChainEntry | None = self._read_last_entry()\n163\t\n164\t    # --- segment layout / manifest ------------------------------------------------\n165\t    def _resolve_active_path(self) -> Path:\n166\t        \"\"\"The current append/read target. With a manifest, the active segment's absolute path; without\n167\t        one (a fresh or not-yet-migrated legacy spine), the legacy single file in place — byte-identical\n168\t        to the pre-rotation store, so nothing destructive happens until an explicit `migrate()`.\"\"\"\n169\t        if self._manifest is not None:\n170\t            act = self._manifest.active()\n171\t            if act is not None:\n172\t                return self._layout.seg_path(act)\n173\t        return self.path\n174\t\n175\t    @contextmanager\n176\t    def _crossproc_lock(self) -> \"Iterator[None]\":\n177\t        \"\"\"Cross-process exclusion on the inode-stable lockfile (invariant 14 / D3). flock binds to the\n178\t        open file DESCRIPTION, not the path, so an os.replace of the manifest/segments cannot move the\n179\t        lock out from under a concurrent writer — which the prior flock-on-the-data-fd could not survive.\n180\t        Best-effort exactly as before (fcntl absent on non-POSIX; a flock failure is swallowed).\"\"\"\n181\t        if fcntl is None:  # pragma: no cover — non-POSIX\n182\t            yield\n183\t            return\n184\t        self._layout.lockfile_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n185\t        fd = os.open(str(self._layout.lockfile_path), os.O_RDWR | os.O_CREAT, 0o600)\n186\t        try:\n187\t            try:\n188\t                fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX)\n189\t            except OSError:  # pragma: no cover\n190\t                pass\n191\t            yield\n192\t        finally:\n193\t            os.close(fd)\n194\t\n195\t    def _refresh_active_under_lock(self) -> None:\n196\t        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`. Re-resolve the active segment from the manifest so an appender\n197\t        constructed BEFORE a concurrent migrate()/rotation writes to the CURRENT active target, never a\n198\t        stale path — which would fork the log or resurrect a renamed-away file (the split-brain the\n199\t        two-step migration guards against). Cheap: the manifest is tiny (Slice 1 gates this on a change\n200\t        token).\"\"\"\n201\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n202\t        new_active = self.path\n203\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n204\t        if act is not None:\n205\t            new_active = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n206\t        if new_active != self._active or (m is not None) != (self._manifest is not None):\n207\t            self._manifest = m\n208\t            self._active = new_active\n209\t            self._invalidate_index()\n210\t            self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n211\t\n212\t    def _invalidate_index(self) -> None:\n213\t        \"\"\"MUST hold `_index_lock` OR be on a path with no concurrent index use. Drops the single-segment\n214\t        index so the next read rebuilds it against the (possibly changed) active segment.\"\"\"\n215\t        with self._index_lock:\n216\t            self._index_built = False\n217\t            self._offsets = {}\n218\t            self._max_seq = -1\n219\t            self._scan_pos = 0\n220\t\n221\t    def _reconcile_orphan_migration(self) -> None:\n222\t        \"\"\"Close the migrate() crash window. migrate() does an ATOMIC `os.replace(spine.jsonl →\n223\t        segments/seg-00000000.jsonl)` and THEN publishes the manifest; a crash BETWEEN them leaves seg-0\n224\t        present, `spine.jsonl` gone, and no manifest — at which point the legacy fallback (active =\n225\t        spine.jsonl) would read the spine as EMPTY and silently hide all history. The rename is atomic and\n226\t        already durable, so if we observe exactly that state we simply finish the interrupted migration by\n227\t        publishing the manifest (idempotent, under the cross-process lock). (os.replace is atomic, so\n228\t        spine.jsonl and seg-0 are never both present or both absent from a torn rename — this one state is\n229\t        the whole window.)\"\"\"\n230\t        if self._manifest is not None:\n231\t            return\n232\t        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n233\t        if not (seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists()):\n234\t            return\n235\t        with self._crossproc_lock():\n236\t            if read_manifest(self._layout) is None and seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists():\n237\t                write_manifest(self._layout,\n238\t                               initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=f\"segments/{segment_filename(0)}\"))\n239\t                _log.warning(\"spine: completed an interrupted migration — published the manifest for %s\", seg0)\n240\t            self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n241\t\n242\t    def migrate(self) -> bool:\n243\t        \"\"\"Move a legacy single-file spine into the segment layout: rename `spine.jsonl` →\n244\t        `segments/seg-00000000.jsonl` (atomic, same filesystem — O(1), no byte copy) and publish a\n245\t        single-active-segment manifest. Idempotent (a no-op once a manifest exists). Returns True iff it\n246\t        migrated. SPLIT-BRAIN SAFETY: run this only after every spine writer is on code that flocks the\n247\t        path-stable lockfile (this Slice-0 code) and re-resolves the active segment under that lock — so\n248\t        no old-style appender writes to `spine.jsonl` through the former data-fd flock while this runs.\n249\t        After migration `spine.jsonl` is absent and is NEVER re-created as a data file (all data I/O\n250\t        targets the active segment).\"\"\"\n251\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n252\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n253\t                if read_manifest(self._layout) is not None:\n254\t                    self._refresh_active_under_lock()\n255\t                    return False\n256\t                self._layout.segments_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n257\t                seg0_rel = f\"segments/{segment_filename(0)}\"\n258\t                seg0_abs = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n259\t                if self.path.exists():\n260\t                    os.replace(self.path, seg0_abs)         # atomic rename (same fs); O(1), no copy\n261\t                else:\n262\t                    seg0_abs.touch()                        # fresh spine: create the empty active segment\n263\t                fsync_dir(self._layout.segments_dir)\n264\t                write_manifest(self._layout, initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=seg0_rel))\n265\t                self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n266\t                self._active = self._resolve_active_path()\n267\t                self._invalidate_index()\n268\t                self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n269\t                return True\n270\t\n271\t    # --- write --------------------------------------------------------------------\n272\t    def append(\n273\t        self, *, kind: str, source: str, actor: str, payload: dict[str, Any],\n274\t        parent_id: int | None = None, supersedes_id: int | None = None,\n275\t        ts: str | None = None,\n276\t    ) -> int:\n277\t        content = {\n278\t            \"scope\": SCOPE, \"kind\": kind, \"source\": source, \"actor\": actor,\n279\t            \"payload\": payload, \"parent_id\": parent_id, \"supersedes_id\": supersedes_id,\n280\t        }\n281\t        cert_digest = digest_payload(content)  # wallclock-free\n282\t        # Serialize the whole read-tip → write so concurrent writers (threaded bridge server, gesture\n283\t        # daemon) can't both fork off a stale tip and break the chain. The in-process RLock is keyed on\n284\t        # the STABLE `self.path` (unchanged), so the check-then-append gate `envelope.consume` builds on\n285\t        # top of it stays atomic across a rotation. Re-read the TRUE tip from disk under the lock.\n286\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n287\t            with self._crossproc_lock():                 # cross-process guard on the path-stable lockfile\n288\t                self._refresh_active_under_lock()        # pick up a concurrent migrate()/rotation; never fork\n289\t                active = self._active\n290\t                # binary append+read: lets us TRUNCATE a torn tail before writing (BLOCK-1 fix). `a+b`\n291\t                # creates the file if absent and — in append mode — every write still lands at EOF.\n292\t                with active.open(\"a+b\") as f:\n293\t                    # BLOCK-1: an interrupted write can leave torn bytes PAST the last valid record. Appending\n294\t                    # after them would MERGE (torn + new) into one unparseable line → the new record is silently\n295\t                    # lost, verify() stays green, and a lost kill-switch panic never halts the mesh. Truncate the\n296\t                    # dead tail back to the last valid record FIRST (this only removes never-committed garbage\n297\t                    # from an interrupted write; committed records are untouched). Evaluated on the ACTIVE\n298\t                    # segment under the lock (invariant 7 — the append target is always the true chain tip).\n299\t                    clean_end, last = _last_valid_boundary(active)\n300\t                    pre_size = os.fstat(f.fileno()).st_size\n301\t                    if pre_size > clean_end:\n302\t                        f.truncate(clean_end)\n303\t                        _log.warning(\"spine: truncated a %d-byte torn tail (interrupted write) before append (%s)\",\n304\t                                     pre_size - clean_end, active)\n305\t                    entry = append_entry([last], cert_digest) if last else build_chain([cert_digest])[0]\n306\t                    record = {\n307\t                        \"seq\": entry.seq, **content, \"ts\": ts or now_iso(),\n308\t                        \"cert_digest\": cert_digest, \"prev_hash\": entry.prev_hash, \"entry_hash\": entry.entry_hash,\n309\t                    }\n310\t                    line = json.dumps(record, ensure_ascii=False) + \"\\n\"\n311\t                    offset = clean_end                      # after any truncate, EOF == clean_end (where the line lands)\n312\t                    f.write(line.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n313\t                    f.flush()\n314\t                    os.fsync(f.fileno())                    # FIX 3: an ack'd append is durable across a crash\n315\t                    # FIX 1: keep the index current with NO re-scan when it is already exactly up to date.\n316\t                    # If another PROCESS appended in between, `_scan_pos < offset` and we skip here — the next\n317\t                    # read's `_ensure_index` extends from `_scan_pos`, picking up the gap records AND this one.\n318\t                    with self._index_lock:\n319\t                        if self._index_built and self._scan_pos == offset:\n320\t                            self._offsets[entry.seq] = offset\n321\t                            if entry.seq > self._max_seq:\n322\t                                self._max_seq = entry.seq\n323\t                            self._scan_pos = offset + len(line.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n324\t                self._last = entry\n325\t        return entry.seq\n326\t\n327\t    # --- read ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n328\t    def iter_records(self, *, since_seq: int = -1) -> Iterator[SpineRecord]:\n329\t        \"\"\"Records with seq > `since_seq`, in order. FIX 1: for since_seq >= 0 the index seeks straight\n330\t        to the first wanted line (O(records-returned), not O(file)); a full read (since_seq < 0) starts\n331\t        at byte 0 exactly as before. FIX 2: a line that fails to parse or lacks required keys is SKIPPED\n332\t        (a torn tail no longer crashes the read); a torn MIDDLE line becomes a seq gap that verify() fails\n333\t        on, so mid-file tampering is never silently hidden.\"\"\"\n334\t        if not self._active.exists():\n335\t            return\n336\t        start_off = self._start_offset_for(since_seq)\n337\t        with self._active.open(\"rb\") as f:\n338\t            if start_off:\n339\t                f.seek(start_off)\n340\t            for raw in f:\n341\t                if not raw.strip():\n342\t                    continue\n343\t                try:\n344\t                    rec = SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(raw))\n345\t                except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n346\t                    _log.warning(\"spine: skipping malformed line during iter_records (%s)\", self._active)\n347\t                    continue\n348\t                if rec.seq > since_seq:\n349\t                    yield rec\n350\t\n351\t    def get(self, seq: int) -> SpineRecord | None:\n352\t        \"\"\"A single record by seq — O(1) via the index (FIX 1), byte-identical to a full scan.\"\"\"\n353\t        if seq < 0 or not self._active.exists():\n354\t            return None\n355\t        self._ensure_index()\n356\t        with self._index_lock:\n357\t            off = self._offsets.get(seq)\n358\t        if off is not None:\n359\t            with self._active.open(\"rb\") as f:\n360\t                f.seek(off)\n361\t                raw = f.readline()\n362\t            try:\n363\t                rec = SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(raw))\n364\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n365\t                return None\n366\t            return rec if rec.seq == seq else None\n367\t        # not indexed (beyond the tip, or a corrupt line was skipped) — bounded fallback (still index-seeked)\n368\t        for r in self.iter_records(since_seq=seq - 1):\n369\t            if r.seq == seq:\n370\t                return r\n371\t            if r.seq > seq:\n372\t                break\n373\t        return None\n374\t\n375\t    def tail(self, n: int) -> list[SpineRecord]:\n376\t        \"\"\"The last `n` records, seek-from-end (O(n) bytes, NOT O(file)) — for a bounded RECENT-window\n377\t        read on a large spine. Fewer than `n` if the spine is shorter. NOTE: a bounded window collapses\n378\t        a RAPID (recent) replay flood, but does NOT bound AGGREGATE replay bloat — a rotated pool of\n379\t        distinct bodies larger than the window each ages out and re-records. Only pair `tail()`-based\n380\t        dedup with a record-time freshness gate (or an independent bound); do not rely on it alone to\n381\t        close a bloat sink.\"\"\"\n382\t        if n <= 0 or not self._active.exists():\n383\t            return []\n384\t        with self._active.open(\"rb\") as f:\n385\t            f.seek(0, os.SEEK_END)\n386\t            pos = f.tell()\n387\t            buf = b\"\"\n388\t            while pos > 0 and buf.count(b\"\\n\") <= n:\n389\t                step = min(65536, pos)\n390\t                pos -= step\n391\t                f.seek(pos)\n392\t                buf = f.read(step) + buf\n393\t        recs: list[SpineRecord] = []\n394\t        for ln in [x for x in buf.split(b\"\\n\") if x.strip()][-n:]:\n395\t            try:\n396\t                recs.append(SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(ln)))\n397\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n398\t                continue\n399\t        return recs\n400\t\n401\t    @property\n402\t    def next_seq(self) -> int:\n403\t        return (self._last.seq + 1) if self._last else 0\n404\t\n405\t    def count(self) -> int:\n406\t        return sum(1 for _ in self.iter_records())\n407\t\n408\t    # --- integrity ----------------------------------------------------------------\n409\t    def entries(self) -> list[ChainEntry]:\n410\t        return [\n411\t            ChainEntry(seq=r.seq, prev_hash=r.prev_hash, cert_digest=r.cert_digest, entry_hash=r.entry_hash)\n412\t            for r in self.iter_records()\n413\t        ]\n414\t\n415\t    def verify(self) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n416\t        \"\"\"Two-layer UNKEYED integrity: (1) BINDING — each record's payload still hashes to its\n417\t        stored cert_digest (catches silent payload edits); (2) CHAIN — the entries link cleanly\n418\t        (catches delete/reorder/entry tamper). This proves internal CONSISTENCY, not authenticity:\n419\t        a naive payload edit fails (1), and a mid-chain digest edit cascades an entry_hash/prev_hash\n420\t        break caught by (2) — BUT a writer who recomputes cert_digest+entry_hash for the tip (no\n421\t        successor to cascade into) or forward-cascades a fork produces a self-consistent chain that\n422\t        passes here. Resistance to a recompute-capable writer is the owner-SIGNED head's job\n423\t        (`checkpoint.verify_checkpoint`, Ed25519 + monotonic last_seq). Use this for corruption/\n424\t        naive-tamper detection; use the signed head for tamper-EVIDENCE.\"\"\"\n425\t        entries: list[ChainEntry] = []\n426\t        for r in self.iter_records():\n427\t            content = {\n428\t                \"scope\": r.scope, \"kind\": r.kind, \"source\": r.source, \"actor\": r.actor,\n429\t                \"payload\": r.payload, \"parent_id\": r.parent_id, \"supersedes_id\": r.supersedes_id,\n430\t            }\n431\t            if digest_payload(content) != r.cert_digest:\n432\t                return False, f\"binding break at seq {r.seq}: payload does not match cert_digest (record tampered)\"\n433\t            entries.append(ChainEntry(seq=r.seq, prev_hash=r.prev_hash, cert_digest=r.cert_digest, entry_hash=r.entry_hash))\n434\t        return verify_chain(entries)\n435\t\n436\t    def _read_last_entry(self) -> ChainEntry | None:\n437\t        # `_last_nonempty_line` skips a torn/garbage tail and returns the last VALID line (FIX 2), so a\n438\t        # crash mid-write can no longer block a restart. The returned line is guaranteed parseable.\n439\t        line = _last_nonempty_line(self._active) if self._active.exists() else None\n440\t        if not line:\n441\t            return None\n442\t        d = json.loads(line)\n443\t        return ChainEntry(seq=d[\"seq\"], prev_hash=d[\"prev_hash\"], cert_digest=d[\"cert_digest\"], entry_hash=d[\"entry_hash\"])\n444\t\n445\t    # --- seq -> byte-offset index (FIX 1) -----------------------------------------\n446\t    def _start_offset_for(self, since_seq: int) -> int:\n447\t        \"\"\"Byte offset to seek so a forward read yields exactly seq > since_seq. A full read\n448\t        (since_seq < 0) starts at 0 WITHOUT building the index — full scans stay a single byte-identical\n449\t        pass. Otherwise the index gives an O(1) seek to the line of seq (since_seq + 1).\"\"\"\n450\t        if since_seq < 0:\n451\t            return 0\n452\t        self._ensure_index()\n453\t        with self._index_lock:\n454\t            off = self._offsets.get(since_seq + 1)\n455\t            if off is not None:\n456\t                return off\n457\t            if since_seq + 1 > self._max_seq:\n458\t                return self._scan_pos              # nothing beyond the tip → the read yields nothing\n459\t        return 0                                    # below the min, or a skipped/corrupt gap → safe full scan\n460\t\n461\t    def _ensure_index(self) -> None:\n462\t        \"\"\"Build the index lazily (one unavoidable scan) and keep it current. Detects a file that GREW\n463\t        (our own or another PROCESS's appends — append-only ⇒ offsets never move, so we only EXTEND) and\n464\t        a file that SHRANK / was rewritten in place smaller (rebuild). Thread-safe under `_index_lock`.\"\"\"\n465\t        try:\n466\t            size = self._active.stat().st_size if self._active.exists() else 0\n467\t        except OSError:\n468\t            return\n469\t        with self._index_lock:\n470\t            if not self._index_built:\n471\t                self._offsets = {}\n472\t                self._max_seq = -1\n473\t                self._scan_pos = 0\n474\t                self._index_built = True\n475\t                self._scan_from(0, size)\n476\t            elif size > self._scan_pos:\n477\t                self._scan_from(self._scan_pos, size)      # extend forward over the newly-appended bytes\n478\t            elif size < self._scan_pos:\n479\t                self._offsets = {}                          # truncated/rewritten smaller → rebuild\n480\t                self._max_seq = -1\n481\t                self._scan_pos = 0\n482\t                self._scan_from(0, size)\n483\t\n484\t    def _scan_from(self, start: int, size: int) -> None:\n485\t        \"\"\"Index every COMPLETE (newline-terminated) record line in bytes [start, size). MUST hold\n486\t        `_index_lock`. A malformed complete line is left OUT of the index (so a mid-file gap still\n487\t        surfaces via verify()); a trailing partial line (no newline yet) is left for the next extend.\"\"\"\n488\t        if size <= start:\n489\t            return\n490\t        with self._active.open(\"rb\") as f:\n491\t            f.seek(start)\n492\t            chunk = f.read(size - start)\n493\t        consumed = 0\n494\t        while True:\n495\t            nl = chunk.find(b\"\\n\", consumed)\n496\t            if nl == -1:\n497\t                break                                       # trailing partial line — not complete yet\n498\t            raw = chunk[consumed:nl]\n499\t            line_off = start + consumed\n500\t            consumed = nl + 1\n501\t            if not raw.strip():\n502\t                continue\n503\t            try:\n504\t                d = json.loads(raw)\n505\t                if not all(k in d for k in _REQUIRED_KEYS):\n506\t                    raise KeyError\n507\t                seq = d[\"seq\"]\n508\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n509\t                continue                                    # corrupt complete line — do not index it\n510\t            self._offsets[seq] = line_off\n511\t            if seq > self._max_seq:\n512\t                self._max_seq = seq\n513\t        self._scan_pos = start + consumed\n514\t"}]},"uuid":"0707a8cf-0101-4f59-858e-aff4c0bd57e9","timestamp":"2026-07-19T15:53:50.887Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"74f982a1-a237-4aae-88c5-146c4973d983","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"0648b736-d888-4567-814a-d78966511109","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"58479281-f0fc-437a-b3c2-51154bb8cfd1","agentId":"a95165ff517d07262","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01W4eHfiypEjoTRRSfa2XEhv","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"The spine's segment MANIFEST — the sole linearization point for the retain-all segment set.\n2\t\n3\tThe spine is stored as a sequence of size-bounded, immutable **sealed** segments plus exactly one\n4\t**active** (append) segment; the manifest names them in seq order. It is deliberately UNSIGNED and\n5\tNON-LOAD-BEARING: record order, count, and every tamper decision are re-derived from the segment BYTES\n6\t(via `verify_chain`/`classify_head`), never from the manifest's convenience fields. A doctored manifest\n7\ttherefore fails CLOSED — dropping/reordering an interior segment breaks `prev_hash`/seq contiguity, a\n8\tdropped tail shortens `entries()` (the signed head's `n < entry_count` gate fires), a dropped front fails\n9\tthe genesis check. (A future signed-manifest tier would set `manifest_sig` — reserved `null` — because it\n10\twould then attest pruned ranges; retain-all needs no such trust.)\n11\t\n12\t`SpineLayout` derives the whole on-disk layout from the spine data-file path a `SpineStore` is constructed\n13\twith, so a store on a temp path is fully isolated. This module does no locking — the caller (`SpineStore`)\n14\tholds the cross-process flock while reading/writing/migrating.\n15\t\"\"\"\n16\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n17\t\n18\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n19\tfrom pathlib import Path\n20\t\n21\tfrom pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field\n22\t\n23\tfrom ..reuse.chain import _GENESIS_PREV\n24\tfrom .atomicio import atomic_write_text\n25\t\n26\tSEGMENT_STEM = \"seg-\"\n27\t_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1\n28\t\n29\t\n30\tdef segment_filename(seg_id: int, codec: str = \"none\") -> str:\n31\t    \"\"\"Zero-padded segment file name; `.gz` suffix when gzip-sealed (Slice 4).\"\"\"\n32\t    base = f\"{SEGMENT_STEM}{seg_id:08d}.jsonl\"\n33\t    return base + \".gz\" if codec == \"gzip\" else base\n34\t\n35\t\n36\tclass Segment(BaseModel):\n37\t    \"\"\"One segment in the manifest. `first_prev_hash`/`boundary_hash` are the chain-linkage the reader\n38\t    CROSS-CHECKS against the segment's real first/last records — they are convenience, not authority.\"\"\"\n39\t    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"ignore\")   # forward-compatible: tolerate fields a newer writer added\n40\t    id: int = Field(ge=0)\n41\t    file: str                                    # path RELATIVE to the spine dir, e.g. \"segments/seg-00000000.jsonl\"\n42\t    codec: str = \"none\"                          # \"none\" | \"gzip\"\n43\t    sealed: bool = False\n44\t    first_seq: int = Field(ge=0)\n45\t    last_seq: int | None = None                  # null while active\n46\t    count: int | None = None                     # null while active; CONVENIENCE ONLY — never a tamper input\n47\t    first_prev_hash: str = _GENESIS_PREV         # == the prior segment's boundary_hash (seg 0 == genesis)\n48\t    boundary_hash: str | None = None             # == entry_hash(last_seq); null while active\n49\t    bytes: int = 0\n50\t    sha256: str | None = None                    # at-rest file integrity; NOT a chain digest\n51\t\n52\t\n53\tclass Manifest(BaseModel):\n54\t    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"ignore\")\n55\t    schema_version: int = _SCHEMA_VERSION\n56\t    generation: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)     # monotonic epoch; ++ on EVERY swap — the unified change token\n57\t    scope: str = \"\"\n58\t    segments: list[Segment] = Field(default_factory=list)\n59\t    manifest_sig: str | None = None              # reserved for the deferred signed-manifest tier\n60\t\n61\t    def active(self) -> Segment | None:\n62\t        \"\"\"The single unsealed (append) segment, if any. There is at most one.\"\"\"\n63\t        act = [s for s in self.segments if not s.sealed]\n64\t        return act[-1] if act else None\n65\t\n66\t    def sealed_in_order(self) -> list[Segment]:\n67\t        return sorted((s for s in self.segments if s.sealed), key=lambda s: s.first_seq)\n68\t\n69\t    def ordered(self) -> list[Segment]:\n70\t        \"\"\"All segments in seq order (sealed by first_seq, then the active tail).\"\"\"\n71\t        out = self.sealed_in_order()\n72\t        act = self.active()\n73\t        if act is not None:\n74\t            out.append(act)\n75\t        return out\n76\t\n77\t\n78\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n79\tclass SpineLayout:\n80\t    \"\"\"The on-disk layout derived from a spine data-file path (…/spine.jsonl). Every path is relative to\n81\t    that file's directory so a store on a temp path is isolated from the real ~/.sigil deployment.\"\"\"\n82\t    data_path: Path            # the legacy/identity path (…/spine.jsonl) — the stable lock/identity token\n83\t    spine_dir: Path\n84\t    segments_dir: Path\n85\t    manifest_path: Path\n86\t    lockfile_path: Path\n87\t    trash_dir: Path\n88\t\n89\t    @classmethod\n90\t    def for_path(cls, data_path: Path | str) -> \"SpineLayout\":\n91\t        p = Path(data_path)\n92\t        d = p.parent\n93\t        return cls(\n94\t            data_path=p, spine_dir=d, segments_dir=d / \"segments\",\n95\t            manifest_path=d / \"manifest.json\", lockfile_path=d / \"spine.lock\", trash_dir=d / \"trash\",\n96\t        )\n97\t\n98\t    def seg_path(self, seg: Segment) -> Path:\n99\t        \"\"\"Absolute path of a segment's file (its `file` is stored relative to the spine dir).\"\"\"\n100\t        return self.spine_dir / seg.file\n101\t\n102\t\n103\tdef read_manifest(layout: SpineLayout) -> Manifest | None:\n104\t    \"\"\"Load the manifest, or None if absent. A present-but-unparseable manifest RAISES (fail closed) —\n105\t    the atomic write path guarantees it is never torn, so a parse failure means genuine corruption, not a\n106\t    partial write, and must never be silently treated as 'no manifest' (which would strand the segments).\"\"\"\n107\t    mp = layout.manifest_path\n108\t    if not mp.exists():\n109\t        return None\n110\t    return Manifest.model_validate_json(mp.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n111\t\n112\t\n113\tdef write_manifest(layout: SpineLayout, manifest: Manifest) -> None:\n114\t    \"\"\"Atomically publish the manifest (temp→fsync→os.replace→dir-fsync). THE cutover commit instant.\"\"\"\n115\t    layout.manifest_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n116\t    atomic_write_text(layout.manifest_path, manifest.model_dump_json(), prefix=\".manifest-\")\n117\t\n118\t\n119\tdef initial_manifest(scope: str, *, active_file: str, first_seq: int = 0,\n120\t                     first_prev_hash: str = _GENESIS_PREV) -> Manifest:\n121\t    \"\"\"A single-active-segment manifest (generation 0) — the shape produced by a fresh store or a\n122\t    migration of an existing single-file spine.\"\"\"\n123\t    return Manifest(\n124\t        generation=0, scope=scope,\n125\t        segments=[Segment(id=0, file=active_file, codec=\"none\", sealed=False,\n126\t                          first_seq=first_seq, first_prev_hash=first_prev_hash)],\n127\t    )\n128\t"}]},"uuid":"66dc596a-390f-45c3-8760-8dfe7e0a3e0f","timestamp":"2026-07-19T15:54:55.826Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0648b736-d888-4567-814a-d78966511109","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
